3122 AE5E C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3123 AE60 0450  20 	b *r0
3124               	;
3125               	; 	' THE FONT'S COLOUR TABLE WAS 472 BYTES OF ONE REPEATED VALUE, which is a
3126               	; 	' fifth of what the fixed area had left. DEFINE COLOR copies such a table
3127               	; 	' out of ROM into all three screen thirds; writing the constant straight
3128               	; 	' into the colour table does the same job for the price of a loop, and
3129               	; 	' the loop runs once, at boot.
3130               	; 	'
3131               	; 	' The colour table is at >2000 and the VDP mirrors it once per screen
3132               	; 	' third, >800 apart. Both numbers were read out of the GENERATED assembly
3133               	; 	' -- define_color's `ai r0,>2000` and LDIRVM3's `ai r0,>0800` -- rather
3134               	; 	' than assumed, because a wrong base here would paint over the pattern
3135               	; 	' table and the failure would look like corrupt artwork.
3136               	; 	'
3137               	; 	' Paced: a few hundred VDP writes in one frame are silently dropped.
3138               	; 	' THE SCANNER'S COLOURS ARE THREE BLOCKS REPEATED SIXTEEN TIMES EACH, so
3139               	; 	' the table shipped 384 bytes to say 24 bytes' worth. Same trick as the
3140               	; 	' font: write the colour table directly. >2000 is the colour table's base
3141               	; 	' and >800 the stride between screen thirds -- the same layout DEFINE COLOR
3142               	; 	' walks for us in setup_font, which this cannot use because it is cheaper to
3143               	; 	' expand 24 bytes here than to ship 384 in the bank.
3144               	; 	' NOTHING IS UNDER THE GROUND FLOOR, SO NOTHING SHOULD BE GREEN THERE.
3145               	; 	' A floor bar is five pixels of yellow over three of the green air
3146               	; 	' belonging to the floor BELOW it (see SLAB), which is right for three of
3147               	; 	' the four bars and wrong for the last one: below floor 0 there is no
3148               	; 	' floor, only the scanner, and the green read as a strip of shop with
3149               	; 	' nothing in it.
3150               	; 	'
3151               	; 	' NO NEW CHARACTER IS NEEDED, BECAUSE THE COLOUR TABLE HAS THREE COPIES
3152               	; 	' -- one per eight screen rows -- and the bands land such that each bar
3153               	; 	' sits in a different third:
3154               	; 	'
3155               	; 	'     band 0 roof   rows  1-5    bar row  5   third 0
3156               	; 	'     band 1        rows  6-10   bar row 10   third 1
3157               	; 	'     band 2        rows 11-15   bar row 15   third 1
3158               	; 	'     band 3 GROUND rows 16-20   bar row 20   third 2
3159               	; 	'
3160               	; 	' so recolouring SLAB in third 2 alone reaches the ground floor's bar and
3161               	; 	' nothing else. The same third also holds the scanner, but the scanner
3162               	; 	' does not use these characters. This is the same mechanism that once hid
3163               	; 	' a bug for months (CLAUDE.md 3A, the blanked-in-one-third note) used
3164               	; 	' deliberately for once.
3165               	; 	'
3166               	; 	' Only the three bottom lines change, and only their BACKGROUND: SLAB has
3167               	; 	' no ink there and SLABE's brick keeps its grey.
3168               	; floor0_colour:
3169               cvb_FLOOR0_COLOUR
3170               	; 	' TWO CHARACTERS, THE SAME THREE SCAN LINES, ONE ROUTINE. Both blocks
3171               	; 	' were written out in full and differed only in which character and which
3172               	; 	' colour byte -- eleven statements twice, including the multiply-by-eight
3173               	; 	' done by doubling (`*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, which clobbers r0,
3174               	; 	' CLAUDE.md 3A).
3175               	; 	f0c = CH_SLAB
3176 AE62 0200  20 	li r0,24576   ; 96*256
     AE64 6000     
3177 AE66 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AE68 20CC     
3178               	; 	f0v = 177			' LYELL on BLACK -- no ink, so the
3179 AE6A 0200  20 	li r0,45312   ; 177*256
     AE6C B100     
3180 AE6E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AE70 20D4     
3181               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows			' background is all that shows
3182 AE72 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AE74 A980     
3183 AE76 AE92     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3184               	; 	f0c = CH_SLABE
3185 AE78 0200  20 	li r0,24832   ; 97*256
     AE7A 6100     
3186 AE7C D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AE7E 20CC     
3187               	; 	f0v = 225			' GRAY brick on BLACK
3188 AE80 0200  20 	li r0,57600   ; 225*256
     AE82 E100     
3189 AE84 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AE86 20D4     
3190               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows
3191 AE88 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AE8A A980     
3192 AE8C AE92     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3193               	; 	RETURN

3576               	;
3577               	; 	' ------------------------------------------- ALPHA LOCK, CALIBRATED
3578               	; 	' On the TI, ALPHA LOCK shares a line with the joystick's VERTICAL axis.
3579               	; 	' Latched down it reports a direction that is NEVER RELEASED. Every other
3580               	; 	' game in this repo dodges this by not reading up/down at all; this one
3581               	; 	' cannot, because down is the duck and up is the elevator.
3582               	; 	'
3583               	; 	' THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS REFUSED TO START UNTIL THE AXIS CLEARED, AND
3584               	; 	' THAT WAS THE WRONG CALL. Classic99 defaults to invertcaps=1, so the TI
3585               	; 	' sees ALPHA LOCK DOWN when the host's Caps Lock is UP -- the normal
3586               	; 	' state. The game then sat on its title screen for ever waiting for a
3587               	; 	' condition the player had no reason to suspect, which presents as "the
3588               	; 	' title comes up and it will not start". A check that turns a survivable
3589               	; 	' input quirk into a dead game is worse than no check.
3590               	; 	'
3591               	; 	' So: sample the axis for 40 frames BEFORE any input can reasonably have
3592               	; 	' been given. A direction held for essentially all of them is not a player
3593               	; 	' -- it is the key. Record it, say so, and then IGNORE that direction for
3594               	; 	' the whole game, which makes ALPHA LOCK harmless instead of fatal.
3595               	; alock_cal:
3596               cvb_ALOCK_CAL
3597               	; 	alku = 0

3808               	; 	' FIRE ONLY, AND THAT IS A DECISION RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.
3809               	; 	'
3810               	; 	' `1` used to start the game as well, because on the TI joystick fire is
3811               	; 	' TAB -- neither guessable nor forgiving, since Windows treats a stray TAB
3812               	; 	' as a focus change and moves the whole window away. CLAUDE.md 3A still
3813               	; 	' says not to make FIRE the only way out of a title screen, and this screen
3814               	; 	' is the named exception.
3815               	; 	'
3816               	; 	' WHAT IT BUYS IS THE 838 SEQUENCE. Every digit on this screen now means
3817               	; 	' exactly one thing: a step of the cheat code, or a reset of it. While `1`
3818               	; 	' also meant START, mistyping one digit of 8-3-8 did not merely reset the
3819               	; 	' sequence -- it began a game, which is a far worse outcome than having to
3820               	; 	' type the code again, and it is the single most likely typo since 1 is
3821               	; 	' next to nothing on the sequence but adjacent to everything on a keypad.
3822               	; 	'
3823               	; 	' The hazard the digit guarded against is real but recoverable: a TAB that
3824               	; 	' moves focus leaves the title on screen and the player clicks back. A
3825               	; 	' game started by accident cannot be undone at all.
3826               	; 	IF cont1.button THEN RETURN

3843               	; 	' wanted next. That worked -- but only because 8-3-8 ALTERNATES, so holding
3844               	; 	' 8 cannot advance past the first step. It is an accident of the sequence
3845               	; 	' rather than a design, and it had two costs: 8,5,3,9,8 opened this page as
3846               	; 	' readily as 8,3,8, and a key that READS as held for many frames got a free
3847               	; 	' walk through the state machine.
3848               	; 	'
3849               	; 	' That second one is not hypothetical on this machine. ALPHA LOCK shorts a
3850               	; 	' keyboard line -- and Classic99 defaults to invertcaps, so it reads DOWN
3851               	; 	' with the host's Caps Lock UP -- and this page was reached twice from
3852               	; 	' single keypresses while testing an unrelated change.
3853               	; 	'

3855               	; 	' Bust-A-Bobble resets only on a stray 3 and still lets 8,5,3,8 through.

     B730 20F8     
4225               	;
4226               	; 	' HARRY STARTS ON FLOOR 2 AT THE WEST EDGE OF SCREEN 7, and that is as
4227               	; 	' far left as the clock allows. The research says "the second-floor
4228               	; 	' elevator door", which is screen 3, and it cannot be done:
4229               	; 	'
4230               	; 	'   spawn      escape     round
4231               	; 	'   screen 3   119.9 s    100 s   -- impossible
4232               	; 	'   screen 5   108.1 s    100 s   -- impossible
4233               	; 	'   screen 6   102.3 s    100 s   -- impossible
4234               	; 	'   screen 7    96.4 s    100 s   -- 3.6 s of room
4235               	; 	'
4236               	; 	' HE STARTS AT THE LIFT NOW, AND THAT COST HIM A SPEED CHANGE.
4237               	; 	'
4238               	; 	' His escape route ZIGZAGS -- east along floor 2 to its escalator, WEST
4239               	; 	' along floor 3 to that one, then east again along the roof to the door.
4240               	; 	' From the lift on screen 3 that is 5,120 px against the 4,208 he walked
4241               	; 	' from screen 7, so at his old 1.75 px a pass he needed 105 s of a 100 s
4242               	; 	' round: he could never escape at all, and one of the two loss conditions
4243               	; 	' would quietly have stopped existing. This block used to say exactly that
4244               	; 	' and conclude the lift was impossible. It was impossible AT THAT SPEED.
4245               	; 	'
4246               	; 	' AND QUARTER-PIXELS COULD NOT EXPRESS THE ANSWER. A quarter of a pixel
4247               	; 	' per pass is worth about TEN SECONDS of his route, so the two candidate
4248               	; 	' values straddled the buzzer with nothing in between:
4249               	; 	'
4250               	; 	'   2.00 px/pass   escapes at 102.4 s of a 100 s round   never escapes
4251               	; 	'   2.25 px/pass   escapes at  91.0 s                    nine seconds early
4252               	; 	'
4253               	; 	' Worse, 2.25 was not reachable at all: the accumulator below had two
4254               	; 	' drain steps and therefore a hard ceiling of 2 px a pass, so hsp4 = 9
4255               	; 	' silently delivered 2.00 and he fell two thirds of a screen short of his
4256               	; 	' own escape. See the accumulator comment for the full account.
4257               	; 	'
4258               	; 	' Sixteenths with three drains put the step at about 2.5 s instead of 10,
4259               	; 	' and hsp64 = 35 (2.1875 px/pass) lands him at 96.3 s of the 100 --
4260               	; 	' escaping as the timer expires, which is what was asked for.
4261               	; 	'
4262               	; 	' 35 AND NOT 34, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE PLAYER'S OWN SCREEN. Measured
4263               	; 	' in play at 34: standing on an ordinary screen he escaped with one timer
4264               	; 	' unit left, and standing on the ELEVATOR screen he ran out of clock --
4265               	; 	' same route, same constant, decided by where the player happened to be.
4266               	; 	' Everything that moves is paced per loop PASS while the clock is paced in
4267               	; 	' real FRAMES, so a busy screen slows Harry in real time and the clock does
4268               	; 	' not slow with him. A whole round measured 2,335 passes over about 98 s --
4269               	; 	' 23.8 a second, not the 25 the model assumes -- and the heavy screens are
4270               	; 	' slower again. 35 buys the margin that covers the worst of them. The
4271               	; 	' underlying split is recorded in DESIGN.md 0f-ter; this constant only
4272               	; 	' papers over it.
4273               	; 	'
4274               	; 	' The margin
4275               	; 	' that remains is the ROUND's, not the chase's: Kelly still has to get
4276               	; 	' there first, and does. assets/checkchase.py walks both routes out of
4277               	; 	' these constants, models the drain ceiling, and fails the build if the
4278               	; 	' configured speed is one the accumulator cannot deliver.
4279               	; 	hlv = 1
4280 B732 0200  20 	li r0,256   ; 1*256
     B734 0100     
4281 B736 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_HLV
     B738 2191     
4282               	; 	hsc = 3
4283 B73A 0200  20 	li r0,768   ; 3*256
     B73C 0300     
4284 B73E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_HSC

4439               	;
4440               	; 	' HARRY'S SPEED IS IN QUARTER PIXELS, AND IT DOES NOT RAMP. It used to be
4441               	; 	' 2 px/frame against Kelly's 3, which sounds like a comfortable 1.5x --
4442               	; 	' and Kelly still lost the race to the roof by ELEVEN SECONDS, every
4443               	; 	' round, because the two routes are not the same length. Kelly runs
4444               	; 	' 7,992 px (three traverses plus the roof); Harry runs 4,104, because he
4445               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase
4446               	; 	' resolves on PATH / SPEED, and Kelly needs to be more than TWICE
4447               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.
4448               	; 	'
4449               	; 	' 1.75, AND THE NUMBER IS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Tracked off the
4450               	; 	' reference video (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen
4451               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4452               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.
4453               	; 	'
4454               	; 	' 2.0 would match the original exactly and still cannot be used, because
4455               	; 	' OUR routes are not the original's: Kelly's is 1.95x Harry's here, so a
4456               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves him 5.2 s ahead at the escape edge -- less
4457               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a
4458               	; 	' hit. That figure counts the LIFT, which is on his way and carries him
4459               	; 	' two floors; the foot-only route is 5 s worse and is not the route
4460               	; 	' anybody takes.
4461               	; 	'
4462               	; 	' THE PER-KROOK DIAL IS STILL NOT HIS SPEED. It is the obstacles, which
4463               	; 	' is also the only dial the manual names (`obsp` above, and the ball
4464               	; 	' arcs). assets/checkchase.py checks all of this mechanically -- run it
4465               	; 	' if you touch these numbers.
4466               	; 	hsp64 = 57			' 0.891 px per FRAME, every Krook

4491 B8B6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4492 B8B8 0450  20 	b *r0
4493               	;
4494               	; 	' ======================================================================
4495               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4496               	; 	' ======================================================================
4497               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4498               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4499               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4500               	; 	' of running.
4501               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4502               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4503               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4504               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4505               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4506               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4507               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4508               	; 	'
4509               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4510               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4511               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4512               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4513               	; 	'
4514               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4515               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4516               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4517               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4518               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4519               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4520               	; 	'
4521               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4522               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4523               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4524               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4525               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4526               	; 	'
4527               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4528               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4529               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4530               	; draw_screen:
4531               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4532               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4533 B8BA 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8BC A980     
4534 B8BE E9E8     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY
4535               	; 	GOSUB load_band
4536 B8C0 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8C2 A980     
4537 B8C4 BCAE     	data cvb_LOAD_BAND
4538               	; 	FOR dq = 0 TO 3
4539 B8C6 04C0  14 	clr r0
4540 B8C8 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DQ
     B8CA 21A4     
4541               cv125
4542               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4543               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4544               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4545               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4546               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4547               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4548               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4549               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4550               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4551               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.
4552               	; 		WAIT

4638 B9A4 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0

4640               	;
4641               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4642               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4643               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4644               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4645               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4646               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4647               	; 	'
4648               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4649               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4650               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4651               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4652               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4653               	; 	'
4654               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4655               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4656               	; 	' above it at all.
4657               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4658               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4659               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4660               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4661               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4662               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4663               	; 	'
4664               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4665               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4666               	; beam_one:
4667               cvb_BEAM_ONE

5201               	; 			' AND HOW MANY FLOORS CARRY ONE AT ALL. The gates above
5202               	; 			' decide what KIND a hazard is; this decides whether the
5203               	; 			' floor has one.

5205               	; 			' The kind-arrival rule downgrades anything that has not
5206               	; 			' arrived yet to a beach ball rather than leaving the floor

5208               	; 			' Krook 1 was a ball on all FOUR floors of every populated
5209               	; 			' screen. All four bands are on screen at once, so that is
5210               	; 			' four identical balls in view at all times, which is not
5211               	; 			' what "merely a few beach balls" describes.
5212               	; 			'
5213               	; 			' Per-band count was never the problem: only one slot is
5214               	; 			' live until Krook 6. It is the number of OCCUPIED FLOORS,
5215               	; 			' and it had no ramp at all.
5216               	; 			'
5217               	; 			' Floors 1 and 3 stay clear on Krook 1, floor 1 joins on
5218               	; 			' Krook 2, and the roof on Krook 3. ALTERNATING rather than
5219               	; 			' clearing the top or bottom half, so the empty floors do

5261               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
5262               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
5263               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
5264               	; 			'
5265               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
5266               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
5267               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
5268               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
5269               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
5270               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
5271               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
5272               	; 			'
5273               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
5274               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
5275               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
5276               	; 			'
5277               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
5278               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs

5356               cv204
5357               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
5358               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5359               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5360               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5361               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5362               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.
5363               	; 			'
5364               	; 			' The table's x becomes a stagger from that edge rather than
5365               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads them out
5366               	; 			' so they arrive in sequence instead of as one wall.
5367               	; 			'
5368               	; 			' THE SECOND ONE IS A FIXED DISTANCE BEHIND THE FIRST, AND
5369               	; 			' THAT DISTANCE IS SET BY THE JUMP.
5370               	; 			'
5371               	; 			' It used to be `(lx AND 63) + 64` -- a masked byte out of
5372               	; 			' the placement table plus a constant -- so the gap between
5373               	; 			' the two hazards was whatever those two bytes happened to
5374               	; 			' differ by. Measured across the five populated screens it
5375               	; 			' came out 46, 46, 70, 70, 70 px. Screens 2 and 4 flank the
5376               	; 			' elevator, so the store had a visibly tighter pair on one
5377               	; 			' side of it than the other, which is exactly how it was
5378               	; 			' reported from play.
5379               	; 			'
5380               	; 			' Both numbers are too small, which is the real fault. Kelly
5381               	; 			' closes on an oncoming hazard at WALKSP + the hazard's own
5382               	; 			' speed = 6 px a pass, and a jump lasts about 30 passes, so
5383               	; 			' a jump eats ~120 px of closing distance. At 46 or 70 there
5384               	; 			' is NO screen where he can land between the two: he clears
5385               	; 			' the first and comes down on the second.
5386               	; 			'
5387               	; 			' HAZGAP is that distance with margin. The first hazard
5388               	; 			' still lands where the table puts it, so the placement
5389               	; 			' variety is unchanged; only the pairing is now a fact about
5390               	; 			' the jump rather than an accident of two bytes.
5391               	; 			' assets/checkspace.py measures it and fails the build.
5392               	; 			' THE SECOND SLOT IS MEASURED FROM THE FIRST, not from its
5393               	; 			' own table byte. Adding HAZGAP to its own `lx AND 63` would
5394               	; 			' still leave the GAP varying by up to 63 px between screens
5395               	; 			' -- the same fault in a politer form, and the reason the two
5396               	; 			' sides of the elevator differed in the first place. Holding
5397               	; 			' slot 0's stagger and adding to that makes the gap exactly
5398               	; 			' HAZGAP on every screen, while slot 0 still lands wherever
5399               	; 			' the table puts it, so the placement variety is untouched.
5400               	; 			stag = lx AND 63
5401 BEE6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0

5480 BFA4 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5481               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5482               	; 			'
5483               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5484               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5485               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5486               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5487               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5488               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5489               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5490               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5491               	; 			'
5492               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5493               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5494               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5495               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5496               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5497               	; 			'
5498               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5499               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5500               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5501               	; 			'
5502               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5503               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5504               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5505               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5506               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5507               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5508               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5509 BFA6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BFA8 21DD

     C6EE C78C     
6402               cv296
6403               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
6404               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6405               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6406               	; 		'
6407               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6408               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6409               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6410               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6411               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6412               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6413               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6414               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6415               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6416               	; 		'
6417               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6418               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6419               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6420               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6421               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6422               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
6423 C6F0 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0

6724               	;
6725               	; 	' --- running
6726               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6727               	; 	'
6728               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6729               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6730               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6731               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6732               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6733               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6734               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6735               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
6736               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
6737               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.

7053               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
7054               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
7055               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
7056               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
7057               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
7058               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
7059               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
7060               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
7061               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
7062               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
7063               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
7064               	; 	'
7065               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
7066               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
7067               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
7068               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
7069               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7629               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7630               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is

7632               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7633               	; 	'
7634               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7635               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7636               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7637               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7638               	; 	'
7639               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7640               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7641               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7642               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7643               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7644               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7645               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7646               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7647               	; 	'
7648               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7649               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7650               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7651               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7652               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7653               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7654               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7655               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7656               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7657               	; 	'
7658               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7659               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7660               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7661               	; wipe_shelf:
7662               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7663               	; 	wsn = 8

7735               	; 	' A PILLAR'S CAP MAY BE CLEARED; AN END WALL'S MAY NOT. beam_one stamps
7736               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
7737               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7738               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7739               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7740               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
7741               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the
7742               	; 	' BUILDING'S OUTSIDE WALL, carrying its brick bond up through the floor
7743               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7744               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7745               	; 	' nothing.
7746               	; 	'
7747               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
7748               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight
7749               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize

7810               	; 	' used to be an unconditional green VPOKE, which is only correct when the

7814               	; 	' then corrected within the same pass, you could watch it happen at round

8331               	; 				' THEY WRAP. They do not turn round at the wall.
8332               	; 				' A bouncing obstacle is a pendulum: it has a near
8333               	; 				' end and a far end, and the player learns to stand
8334               	; 				' at the far one and wait. Wrapping makes the floor
8335               	; 				' a STREAM that keeps arriving from the same side,
8336               	; 				' so standing still is never the answer and the
8337               	; 				' player has to keep moving -- which is the whole
8338               	; 				' shape of the original.
8339               	; 				IF obd(ui) = 0 THEN

8894               	;
8895               	; 	' THE SIXTEENTH-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen
8896               	; 	' (2), so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here
8897               	; 	' and is spent as whole pixels. THREE IFs, not a loop and not a divide:
8898               	; 	' `%` and `/` both compile to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this
8899               	; 	' runs every pass.
8900               	; 	'
8901               	; 	' THE NUMBER OF DRAINS IS A CEILING ON THE SPEED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE
8902               	; 	' POINT OF THIS COMMENT. Each IF can spend one whole pixel, so N drains
8903               	; 	' cap hspd at N px per pass NO MATTER WHAT hsp64 SAYS. The invariant is:
8904               	; 	'
8905               	; 	'     hacc is under 16 on entry, and hsp64 is at most 48,
8906               	; 	'     so hacc is under 64 here and three drains always suffice.
8907               	; 	'
8908               	; 	' KEEP hsp64 <= 48. This was quarter-pixels with TWO drains and the same
8909               	; 	' invariant written the same way -- "hsp4 is at most 8, so it can never
8910               	; 	' need a third" -- and it was correct until somebody set hsp4 = 9 without
8911               	; 	' re-reading it. The crook then ran at a flat 2.0 px/pass while every
8912               	; 	' comment, the design table and assets/checkchase.py all said 2.25, and
8913               	; 	' the surplus quarter leaked into hacc and wrapped the byte every 256
8914               	; 	' passes. Nothing failed; he simply arrived two thirds of a screen short
8915               	; 	' of his own escape. checkchase.py now counts these IFs and refuses a
8916               	; 	' speed the drains cannot deliver.
8917               	; 	'
8918               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8919               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8920               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8921               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8922               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8923               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8924               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8925               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8926               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8927               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8928               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8929               	; 	' flight looking busy.
8930               	; 	'
8931               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
8932               	; 	'
8933               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the

8935               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
8936               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
8937               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
8938               	; 	'
8939               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,

9024               cv575
9025               	;
9026               	; 	' HE WALKS BY REAL TIME, NOT BY LOOP PASS -- one accumulate-and-drain per
9027               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAME rather than per pass. Everything that moves here used to be
9028               	; 	' per pass while the CLOCK is paced by the frame delta, so a busy screen
9029               	; 	' slowed Harry in real seconds and the clock did not slow with him: the
9030               	; 	' same uninterrupted escape finished with nine timer units in hand from a
9031               	; 	' light screen and none at all from the lift screen, decided by where the
9032               	; 	' player happened to be standing. Now he covers the same ground per second
9033               	; 	' wherever anybody is (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
9034               	; 	'
9035               	; 	' A LOOP, NOT A MULTIPLY. `*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, and reading a
9036               	; 	' 16-bit variable straight after one returns the product's HIGH word
9037               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A). fdv is 1..3 here, so this is at most three adds.
9038               	; 	'
9039               	; 	' SIXTY-FOURTHS, AND ONLY TWO DRAINS -- both fall out of accumulating per
9040               	; 	' FRAME instead of per pass. He covers under a pixel a frame, so two drains
9041               	; 	' are ample where the per-pass version needed three; and the finer unit is
9042               	; 	' what makes the speed tunable at all. In sixteenths one step was about six
9043               	; 	' SECONDS of his route, so nothing landed near the buzzer; in sixty-fourths
9044               	; 	' it is about one and a half. Keep hsp64 <= 128: hacc is under 64 on entry,
9045               	; 	' so 64 + 128 stays inside a byte and two drains always suffice.
9046               	; 	'
9047               	; 	' AND THE STEP IS CLAMPED TO THREE FRAMES' WORTH, WHICH IS NOT A DETAIL.
9048               	; 	' Two tests sample his position once a pass and neither interpolates:
9049               	; 	'
9050               	; 	'   ARRIVAL   `hdx < 6` around the escalator or the roof door -- an ELEVEN
9051               	; 	'             pixel window. A step over 10 px can jump clean across it and
9052               	; 	'             he never arrives at all.
9053               	; 	'   THE CATCH `hdd < CATCHR` is 12, a 23-wide band, and Kelly closes 4 of
9054               	; 	'             it himself -- so Harry over ~18 px could pass through the Kop
9055               	; 	'             between two passes without either sample being inside it.
9056               	; 	'
9057               	; 	' FIVE FRAMES, AND THREE WAS TOO FEW. With two drains he moves at most 2 px
9058               	; 	' a frame, so five frames is 10 px -- and to skip the window he would have
9059               	; 	' to start outside it on one side and land outside it on the other, which
9060               	; 	' needs a step of 12. Ten is safe with a pixel to spare, and 10 + 4 = 14
9061               	; 	' against a 23-wide catch band.
9062               	; 	'
9063               	; 	' It was three, and three was the same bug in miniature. The lift screen
9064               	; 	' runs at almost exactly 20 passes a second, which IS fdv = 3, so every
9065               	; 	' momentary hitch there pushed past the clamp and he lost that frame --
9066               	; 	' measured as TIME 02 left on the lift screen against TIME 04 on a light
9067               	; 	' one, where before the real-time change it had been 00 against 09. The
9068               	; 	' clamp only exists for a genuine stall, and at five it only fires there.
9069               	; 	'
9070               	; 	' The ESCALATOR RIDE is deliberately untouched: it returns above this,
9071               	; 	' clocked one step per pass to stay locked to the moving staircase. A rider
9072               	; 	' advanced by the frame delta either drifts off the treads or makes the
9073               	; 	' steps run backwards (CLAUDE.md 3A, DESIGN.md 0f). It is about 3% of his
9074               	; 	' journey.
9075               	; 	'
9076               	; 	' `hanim` still advances by hspd and is still an ODOMETER -- pixels
9077               	; 	' travelled, not time -- so the run cycle stays locked to the ground at any
9078               	; 	' rate. A 9 px step moves it exactly one pose of its 8 px beat.
9079               	; 	hfd = fdv
9080 DA08 D820  54 	movb @cvb_FDV,@cvb_HFD
     DA0A 2120     
     DA0C 216E     
9081               	; 	IF hfd > 5 THEN hfd = 5
9082 DA0E D020  34 	movb @cvb_HFD,r0
     DA10 216E

     DA14 0600

     DA1C DA26

9296               	; 	' THE CYCLE ADVANCES BY PIXELS TRAVELLED, NOT BY TIME. This was `+ fdv`,
9297               	; 	' which is a clock -- and Harry's speed is a QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR, so
9298               	; 	' hspd is 0 on a third of his frames. On every one of those the legs took
9299               	; 	' another step while the ground did not move, which is precisely what
9300               	; 	' scrubbing your feet looks like. It is the classic foot-slip: the legs
9301               	; 	' and the world running off two different clocks.
9302               	; 	'
9303               	; 	' Advancing by hspd locks the stride to the floor -- he cannot take a step
9304               	; 	' without covering ground, or cover ground without taking one, at any

9563 DDEA 0450  20 	b *r0
9564               	;
9565               	; 	' ======================================================================
9566               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9567               	; 	' ======================================================================
9568               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9569               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9570               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9571               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9572               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9573               	; 	'
9574               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9575               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9576               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9577               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9578               	; 	' broken.
9579               	; 	'
9580               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9581               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9582               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9583               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9584               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9585               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9586               	; 	'
9587               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9588               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9589               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9590               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9591               	; coll_obst:
9592               cvb_COLL_OBST
9593               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
9594 DDEC D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DDEE 21A0

     DDF2 0300

9890               	; 	' THE FLOOR CLEARS WHEN YOU ARE HIT, and stays clear until you re-enter
9891               	; 	' the screen. Nine seconds is a heavy penalty on a fifty-unit clock, and
9892               	; 	' taking it while still standing among the things that charged it -- with
9893               	; 	' a second hazard 48 px behind the first -- is how one mistake becomes
9894               	; 	' three. Clearing the row makes the penalty a single event you can walk
9895               	; 	' away from.
9896               	; 	'
9897               	; 	' BOTH SLOTS, WHATEVER IS IN THEM. Radios used to be exempt: they are
9898               	; 	' CHARACTERS stamped into the name table rather than sprites, so zeroing
9899               	; 	' the kind would have stopped them colliding while leaving them plainly
9900               	; 	' visible on the shelf, and a fixture that is still there has to still be
9901               	; 	' there. That was a reason to UNDRAW them, not a reason to keep them --
9902               	; 	' and it left the one hazard that cannot leave on its own as the only one
9903               	; 	' the mercy did not cover. haz_off now takes the characters with it.
9904               	; 	'
9905               	; 	' NOTHING RESTORES IT HERE. load_band repopulates the band from the
9906               	; 	' template, and it runs only on a seam crossing or a round start, so
9907               	; 	' "until the screen is re-entered" costs no state and no timer: leave and
9908               	; 	' come back and the hazards are simply placed again.
9909               	; 	GOSUB haz_gone
9910 E06C 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     E06E A980

10082               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
10083               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook

10152               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
10153               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
10154               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
10155               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
10156               	; 	'
10157               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
10158               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
10159               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
10160               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
10161               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
10162               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
10163               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

10308               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
10309               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
10310               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
10311               	; 		'
10312               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
10313               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
10314               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
10315               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
10316               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.

10598               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10599               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10600               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10601               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10602               	; 	'
10603               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10604               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10605               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10606               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10607               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10608               	; 	'
10609               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10610               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
10611               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
10612               	; 	' is what running looks like.
10613               	; 	' THE TORSO RUNS ON THE SAME TWO BITS AS THE LEGS. With four drawn poses
10614               	; 	' the shoulders and arms swing WITH the stride; the old two-frame body had
10615               	; 	' a clock of its own, which is what read as a flap rather than a run.
10616               	; 	hp = P_HBODY

10692               	; 	' HE IS STRIPED FROM CAP TO HEM. Both stripe colours run the whole upper
10693               	; 	' half, so both boxes span rows 0-15 and neither can be tucked away --
10694               	; 	' affordable only because Kelly's hat moved into a box of its own. His
10695               	; 	' FACE is pushed DOWN instead (hy+3, box 3..18) so it clears the cap
10696               	; 	' rows, where his two stripe boxes plus Kelly's hat and face already
10697               	; 	' make four. Worst line of a meeting: exactly four, nothing dropped.
10698               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
10699               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
10700               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
10701               	; 	'
10702               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
10703               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
10704               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
10705               	; 	' a jump.
10706               	; 	'
10707               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
10708               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
10709               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
10710               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
10711               	; 	'
10712               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
10713               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
10714               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
10715               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
10716               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
10717               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
10718               	; 	'
10719               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
10720               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
10721               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
10722               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
10723               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
10724               	; 	'
10725               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
10726               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
10727               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
10728               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
10729               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.
10730               	; 	IF hanim AND 8 THEN hy = hy - 1

10919               	; 		' THE SCREEN TEST IS WHAT MAKES IT INVISIBLE. Clearing his whole

11199               	;
11200               	; 	' ======================================================================
11201               	; 	' THE SCANNER
11202               	; 	' ======================================================================
11203               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
11204               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
11205               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
11206               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
11207               	; 	'
11208               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
11209               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
11210               	; 	'
11211               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
11212               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
11213               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
11214               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
11215               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
11216               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
11217               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
11218               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
11219               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
11220               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
11221               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
11222               	; esc_tick:
11223               cvb_ESC_TICK
11224               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
11225               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
11226               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
11227               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11228               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
11229 EA70 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EA72 218E     
11230 EA74 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     EA76 0100

11233 EA7C 0460  28 	b @cv797
     EA7E EA94     
11234               cv798

11236 EA80 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EA82 218E     
11237 EA84 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     EA86 0700     
11238 EA88 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11239 EA8A 1A02  14 	jl cv800
11240 EA8C 0460  28 	b @cv799
     EA8E EA94

11242 EA90 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11243 EA92 0450  20 	b *r0
11244               cv799

11246               cv797
11247               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
11248               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
11249               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
11250               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
11251               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
11252               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
11253               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
11254               	; 	'
11255               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
11256               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
11257               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
11258               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
11259               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
11260               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
11261               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
11262               	; 	escp = escp + 1

11277               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
11278               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
11279               	; 	' 7 an east one.

11282               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once

11285               	; 	'

11556 ECEE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11557 ECF0 0450  20 	b *r0
11558               	;
11559               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11560               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11561               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11562               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11563               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11564               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11565               	; 	'
11566               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11567               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11568               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11569               	; 	'
11570               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11571               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11572               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11573               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11574               	; 	'
11575               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11576               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11577               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11578               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11579               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11580               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11581               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.

11587               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row

11637               	; 		' statements, about 320 bytes, and nine VDP writes a floor that nothing

11641               	; 		' original is left behind -- scan_escs is this block plus the colour

11931               	;
11932               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
11933               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
11934               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
11935               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
11936               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
11937               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11938               	; scan_wipe:
11939               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
11940               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
11941               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
11942               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
11943               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
11944               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
11945               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
11946               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
11947               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
11948               	; 	'
11949               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
11950               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
11951               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
11952               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
11953               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
11954               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
11955               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
11956               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
11957               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
11958               	; 	'
11959               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
11960               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
11961               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
11962               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
11963               	; 	'
11964               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
11965               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
11966               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns

12034               	;
12035               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
12036               	; 	'
12037               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
12038               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
12039               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
12040               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
12041               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
12042               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
12043               	; 	'
12044               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
12045               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
12046               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
12047               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
12048               	; 	'
12049               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
12050               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
12051               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
12052               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
12053               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
12054               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
12055               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
12056               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
12057               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
12058               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
12059               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.

12748               	;
12749               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
12750               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
12751               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
12752               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
12753               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
12754               	; 	'
12755               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
12756               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
12757               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
12758               	; 	'
12759               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
12760               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
12761               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
12762               	; 	'
12763               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
12764               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).

12867 F678 0460  28 	b @cvb_BN_LOOP
     F67A F5CC     
12868               	;
12869               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
12870               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
12871               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
12872               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
12873               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
12874               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
12875               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
12876               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
12877               	; 	'
12878               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
12879               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
12880               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12881               	;
12882               	; do_escape:
12883               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
12884               	; 	escapd = 0

12921 F6BC EA32     	data cvb_HIDE_ALL
12922               	; 	' THE MESSAGE BOX, ON THE GAME SCREEN AND NOT INSTEAD OF IT.
12923               	; 	'
12924               	; 	' It used to CLS before GAME OVER, so the last thing the player saw was
12925               	; 	' the store being deleted and then two words on an empty field -- which
12926               	; 	' reads as the program ending rather than as the game ending. The store
12927               	; 	' stays; the message sits in the middle of it.
12928               	; 	'
12929               	; 	' THE BOX IS THE FONT'S OWN BACKGROUND. Every font character is black on
12930               	; 	' HUD_BG (dark blue), so a row of SPACES is a solid dark blue bar. A blank
12931               	; 	' row above and below the text is a frame for the price of two strings and
12932               	; 	' no new characters, and it reads against the store because nothing else on
12933               	; 	' the playfield is that colour.
12934               	; 	'
12935               	; 	' THIRTEEN WIDE, WHICH IS THE LONGEST MESSAGE PLUS ONE EITHER SIDE.
12936               	; 	' HE GOT AWAY and THE BIPLANE are both eleven. Every string below is padded
12937               	; 	' to exactly thirteen so the box has straight edges, and column 10 puts it
12938               	; 	' at columns 10-22 -- centre 16, the screen's.
12939               	; 	'
12940               	; 	' THE REASON IS ALWAYS ON THE SAME ROW, and that is what lets one layout
12941               	; 	' serve both cases. Losing a life draws three rows around it; losing the
12942               	; 	' last one draws two more ON TOP, so GAME OVER appears above the reason
12943               	; 	' rather than replacing it. Printing the reason at a different row for each
12944               	; 	' case would need `PRINT AT` with a variable, and every other PRINT in this
12945               	; 	' program uses a constant.
12946               	; 	PRINT AT 330,"             "

13088               	; 		' globals, so a starting Krook or Kop count typed on the setup
13089               	; 		' screen otherwise applied to every game after it -- including one
13090               	; 		' started by somebody who never saw the screen and had no way to
13091               	; 		' know why they began on level 12 with two Kops. Zeroing them lets
13092               	; 		' new_game's own defaults take over; typing 8-3-8 again sets them

     F856 AA32     
13109               	;
13110               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
13111               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
13112               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
13113               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
13114               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
13115               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
13116               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
13117               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
13118               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
13119               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
13120               	; 	' Harry was caught.
13121               	; snd_off:
13122               cvb_SND_OFF

13574 FC2A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
13575 FC2C 0450  20 	b *r0
13576               	;
13577               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
13578               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
13579               	; 	'
13580               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
13581               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
13582               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
13583               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
13584               	; 	'
13585               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
13586               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
13587               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
13588               	; 	'
13589               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
13590               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
13591               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
13592               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
13593               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
13594               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
13595               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
13596               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
13597               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
13598               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
13599               	; 	#if TI994A
13600               	; 	BANK 1
13601      03D0     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept
